## Document 010: 2026-09-25_［門間一夫の経済深読み］成長戦略の本質的な限界_1429754b

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026-09-25_［門間一夫の経済深読み］成長戦略の本質的な限界_1429754b.pdf

門間 一夫の 
経済深読み

成長戦略の本質的な限界 
みずほ総合研究所  
エグゼクティブエコノミスト   門間一夫 202 6 年 9 月 25 日

絶望的なトラックレコード 
高市政権は「強い経済」の実現に向け、17 の戦
略分野を中心に官民一体で国内投資を進める 1。
供給力を強化して 潜在成長率を引き上げるのが
狙いだ。期待される効果は、内閣府が「中長期の
経済財政に関する試算」（2026 年 7 月）で示して
いる（図表1）。将来の経済成長率は、何もしなけ
れば 0.4～0.5％にとどまるが、 高市政権の成長戦
略で 1.7～1.8％まで高まると言う。初めて見るな
ら希望がわく試算だが、同じような試算は過去に
何度も見た。 
成長戦略は「失われた 10 年」と言われ始めた
2000 年代初頭、小泉政権のもとで本格化し、その
後も看板を掛け変えながら継続されてきた。先ほ
どの中長期試算もそのころからのものだ。小泉政
権では「聖域なき構造改革」が謳われ、経済財政
白書の副題は5 年連続「改革なくして成長なし」
だった。その後も民主党政権で「新成長戦略」が
掲げられ、第 2～4 次の安倍政権では「日本再興
戦略」を皮切りに何バージョンかの成長戦略があ
った。その後の岸田政権では「新しい資本主義」
が標榜された。

1 17 分野は AI・半導体、デジタル・サイバーセキュリティ、情報通信、量子、防衛産業、航空・宇宙、海洋、造船、マ
テリアル（重要鉱物・部素材） 、合成生物学・バイオ、創薬・先端医療、資源・エネルギー安全保障・GX、フュージョン
エネルギー、防災・国土強靱化、港湾ロジスティクス、フードテック、コンテンツ。

### Page 2

2

それらの結果はどうだったのか。内閣府の中長期試算は約25 年にわたり、成長戦略で2％前後ないし
それ以上の成長が可能との姿を描き続けてきた。しかし、事後的な潜在成長率は全くそうならなかった
（図表2）。潜在成長率は経済成長率の実績から推計され、好不況の影響も受ける。途中の上下動を捨象
して大局的にみれば、「2000 年代初頭に 1％程度だった潜在成長率が、近年は 0.5％程度まで低下した」
というのが、歴代成長戦略のトラックレコードである。成長戦略の策定・実行が繰り返された四半世紀
の間に、日本の成長力は半減したということだ。 
目的と手段のあいまいな関係 
成長戦略はなぜ成功しなかったのだろうか。有識者に意見を求めれば、構造改革への踏み込みが甘か
った、政治が弱者や既得権益を守ろうとしすぎた、PDCA サイクルを回せていなかった、金融緩和や財政
支出に頼りすぎた、リスクをとらない民間企業も悪い、などの答えが返ってきそうだ。見当違いだとは
思わないが、 本質的な問題点は、「目的を実現する確かな手段がもともとわかっていなかった」ことに尽
きるのではないか。発展段階を過ぎた日本のような成熟国で「やや野心的な経済成長率」を政策目的と
した時、それを実現する具体的かつ実行可能な政策手段は、存在しないか、探り当てるのがきわめて困
難である。 
抽象論を語るのはたやすい。経済成長のためには、規制緩和、イノベーションの促進、金融市場の改
革、労働市場の改革、人材の育成、財政のワイズスペンディング（賢い支出）などをやればよい。経済
論壇にあふれる主張はほとんどこの類である。しかし、それを具体的な政策 メニューに展開した途端、
「定量効果の不可知性」および「実行上の制約」という二つの壁にぶつかる。 
「定量効果の不可知性」 については、 ミクロとマクロの違いという視点で後ほど整理するが、要は 「マ
クロ経済」というものがあまりに複雑すぎるのである。例えば、何らかの規制緩和を行った時、直接関
連する範囲で何が起きそうかを想像できたとしても、プラスマイナス様々な化学反応の末、経済全体に
どれだけの影響が及ぶのかについて、精度の高い定量的な試算は難しい。「経済成長」 という目的と「規
制緩和」という手段を、確実に結びつけることはできないということだ。先述した内閣府の中長期試算
は、あくまで「ベスト・エフォート」で作られたものであり、もともと相当の幅を持って見なければな
らない試算である。 
高市政権の「責任ある積極財政」についても、「それで成長できる」という見方もあれば、「あまり効
果がない」 「財政への信認を損なうリスクの方が大きい」という声もある。「定量的」 どころか 「定性的」
と言えるほど大きな意見の相違だが、このレベルの論争すらエビデンスで決着をつけることはできない。 
こうしたマクロ経済の複雑さは、政策効果の予測にとどまらず、政策効果の事後検証をも難しいもの
にする。過去の政策のどれかひとつに絞っても、それがもたらした効果や副作用の検証は難しいのに、
成長戦略は様々な政策のパッケージでその中身もよく入れ替わる。その間に外部環境も変化し、金融危
機、大災害、戦争、パンデミックなどがそれなりの頻度で起きる。それらをすべて解きほぐし、いつの
成長戦略のどの部分がいつの経済成長率を何％押し上げたのかについて、 意味のある計算は不可能であ
る。経済成長とそれを実現する手段との関係を、成熟国の政策論に役立つ精度で特定することは、人類

### Page 3

3

の英知を超える作業だと考えるべきだろう。 
成長戦略には「実行上の制約」もある。これも後ほど述べるが、マクロ経済は利害が絡み合う個々の
集合体である。ある政策で得する者はそのメリットを主張し、そうでない者はデメリットを主張する。
そのいずれをも尊重しながら政策の可否を検討すれば、当然ながら実行の範囲やスピードは制約される。
利害調整に役立つと考えられるのが「エビデンスに基づく政策立案」であるが、肝心の経済成長率に与
える影響について強いエビデンスが得られることはない。 
先ほど、成長戦略が成功しなかった理由の事後的な認識も難しい、 と述べた。それでも、「わかりやす
さ」を信条とする経済論壇やメディアは、「過去の成長戦略のここが問題」「だからこうすべきだ」とい
う断定調の論評であふれ返る。政治家はその中から「票になりそう」なテーマをかぎ分け、新しい成長
戦略の柱に仕立て上げる。 
過去 25 年はそういうことの繰り返しだったのではないか。小泉政権では規制緩和に重点が置かれ、
アベノミクスでは金融緩和や資本市場改革が進められた。「新しい資本主義」では人材の育成や賃上げ
の重要性が叫ばれ、高市政権は財政支出に活路を見出そうとしている。肝心の経済成長との関係が本当
はよくわからないまま、政策手段のローテーションだけが続いていく。 
「ミクロ」と「マクロ」の根本的な違い 
個々のレベルでは、野心的な成長を実現できている企業が少なくない。そういう企業は、創意工夫、
人材育成、 市場開拓、 ガバナンスなどの面で優れている場合が多い。しかし、「それを皆でやれば経済が
成長する」ことにはならない。「マクロ」は「ミクロ」を相似的に大きくしたものではなく、異質の「ミ
クロ」が複雑に絡まる集合体だからである。個別事例から得られるマクロへのインサイトが限られるこ
とを、いくつかの視点で確認しておこう。 
第一に、局所的にはかなりの成功と言えるケースでも、経済全体を押し上げるほどの力にはなかなか
ならない。例えば、インバウンド観光の推進は成功例と言ってよい。 小泉政権の「ビジット ・ ジャパン」
キャンペーンに始まり、ビザ発給要件の大幅緩和、空港・LCC の拡充、免税制度の充実など各種施策の
着実な推進もあり、インバウンド観光は2010 年代を中心に急成長した。 今やその規模は10 兆円に迫り、
自動車に次ぐ第2 の産業とも言われる。しかし、それでも前述の通り、日本経済全体の成長力ははっき
り低下した。 
第二に、個々の経済主体は多かれ少なかれ相互に競争関係にある。個別の成功例は、多くの場合「競
争に勝った例」であり、容易に真似できないビジネスモデルを作れた例である。競争社会において「良
い企業を皆で真似ればよい」と考えるのは、それ自体として論理矛盾である。逆に、皆が真似できる程
度の成功ならその成功に持続性はない。 
第三に、一部の経済主体に「追い風」となる環境変化や政策でも、他の経済主体には「逆風」になる
場合がある。インバウンド観光が成長した裏には、政府の施策だけではなく、アジアの購買力拡大や円
安などの追い風もあった。しかし、日本全体でみれば、アジアとの競争あるいは円安に苦しめられてき
た企業も多い。コーポレート・ガバナンス改革も、株価上昇や配当増加の原動力となった一方、事業再

### Page 4

4

編、コスト抑制、合理化などを通じて、少なからぬ労働者や中小企業に厳しい環境をもたらした。 
第四に、ボトルネックの重要性はミクロとマクロでは次元が異なる。例えば、人手不足と言われる状
況でも、魅力的なビジネスモデルや好待遇で人を集められる企業も多い。しかし、マクロでみれば人の
取り合いであり、人口の減少・高齢化が進む限り経済全体の成長は制約される。エネルギーや希少資材
についても同じことが言える。 
第五に、経済成長とそれ以外の価値（例えば環境、格差、安全、既得利益、地域社会）との相対的な
重要性について、人々の間には多様な考え方や利害があり、国全体として何をどれだけ優先すべきかの
自明な解はない。変化を好まない人々をよく「抵抗勢力」と呼ぶが、どの程度の変化を望むかも価値観
であり、簡単に善悪をつけられない場合も少なくない。個別企業の場合なら、経営者のビジョンと責任
で「とがったリスク」を取り、高成長を追うこともできる。とてつもない成功例がたまにあるのは、そ
れが「ミクロ」だからである。しかし、マクロの話になれば、複雑なトレードオフを無視してGDP 優先
というわけにはいかず、ていねいに議論を進めてバランスをとるしかない。毎年の「骨太の方針」が、
各方面に配慮した総花的なものになるのは、民主主義が機能している証拠でもある。 
では政府は何をすべきなのか 
歴代政権の成長戦略は、強力な政治のリーダーシップのもとで、官民が持てるベストの英知を集めて
策定・実行されてきたものだと思う。 そのうえで四半世紀にわたり、「かすりもしない」レベルで成長目
標が未達だったのだから、日本で潜在成長率 2％近くを目的とした政策パッケージは、ことごとく現実
的でないと考えるべきだろう。 
では、 政府はどうするべきなのだろう。25 年ではだめだったが、50 年、100 年やればそのうちできる
と信じ、今後も高い経済成長率を目標に政策を策定し続けるのか。あるいは、経済成長率の目標を現実
的なレベルまで下げるのか （そうすればこれまでの成長戦略もすべて成功だった！）。それとも、成熟段
階に達した国で経済成長を政策の目的とすること自体が、 必ずしも適切なアジェンダ設定とは言えない
のだろうか。難しい論点なので、いずれ改めて考えてみたい。

## Document 014: 2026-10-01_［みずほ経済EXPRESS］対日直接投資拡大で国内供給力の強化へ_f76b5ad3

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026-10-01_［みずほ経済EXPRESS］対日直接投資拡大で国内供給力の強化へ_f76b5ad3.pdf
Report source: Mizuho
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

みずほ経済 EXPRESS

対日直接投資拡大で国内供給力の強化へ 
～戦略 17 分野でみた外資誘致の現状と課題～

調査部  エコノミスト 阿部大樹 
080-3709-6077 taiki.abe@mizuho-rt.co.jp 
■ 高まる国内投資の機運。 国内供給力強化に向けては対内直接投資の取り込みも重要に 
政府は2026年7月、 日本成長戦略会議において、 国内供給力の強化に向けた官民投資ロードマップを
決定した。とりわけ経済安全保障上の重要性や成長期待が高い17の戦略分野においては、2040年度ま
でに累計370兆円超の官民投資が想定されている（図表1）。日本政策投資銀行「2026年度設備投資計
画調査」では、大企業の約58％、中堅企業の約43％が戦略分野における国内投資を強化すると答えて
おり、多くの企業が政府の方針を前向きに受け止めていることがうかがえる。 
国内投資促進の主軸を担うのは政府や国内民間企業だが、国内供給力を強化していくうえでは、外
国資本の取り込み、すなわち対内直接投資の拡大を促すことも重要だ。対内直接投資とは、外国企業
が日本企業への出資や融資を通じて、日本国内の企業経営・事業に継続的に関わることを目的とした
投資のことをいう。政府も対日直接投資推進会議で、戦略分野ごとに有望な外国企業を発掘し誘致す
る方針を掲げている。 
対内直接投資は、 投資形態の違いから四つに分類できる （図表2） 。 このうち、 「合併・買収」 や 「企

が、具体的な想定がわからない分については除外

### Page 2

2

業・財務再編」は国内供給力の強化につながらない可能性もあるが、「グリーンフィールド投資」と
「能力増強投資」は工場・生産ラインなど生産資本の増加をともない、供給網のレジリエンス向上や
国内の供給力強化につながりやすい形態だ。本稿では、これら二つの形態を合わせて「国内供給力強
化投資」と呼ぶこととする。以下では「国内供給力強化投資」に焦点を絞り、戦略分野ごとに直近5年
間の投資流入状況を確認したうえで、対内直接投資の今後の増加余地と誘致のあり方を検討する。 
■ AI・データセンター関連投資等のグローバルな機運が国内供給力強化投資をけん引  
日本の対内直接投資は、2010年代半ば以降、拡大傾向にある。国際収支統計で集計される対内直接
投資の動向を示した図表3をみると、 その力強い伸びが顕著に見て取れる。GDP比でも1.0％ 程 度（2025
年）まで増加しており、外国企業の国内投資志向が高まりつつある証左と言えよう。 
先行きは、さらなる対内直接投資の拡大が期待できそうだ。予定段階の個別投資案件を集計してい
るfDi Marketsのデータで国内供給力強化投資の動向を確認すると、2020年代に入って投資金額が大幅
に拡大していることがわかる （図表4）。同データでみられる投資拡大の動きは、クロスボーダー金融
取引の実績を計上している国際収支統計でも今後顕在化してくるとみられる。 
さらに、個別投資案件のデータを確認することで、国際収支統計では把握できなかった対内直接投
資の性質・内容を詳細に分析することが可能である。戦略分野別に国内供給力強化投資をみると、AI・
半導体分野や、データセンター関連が含まれるデジタル・サイバーセキュリティ分野で投資金額が大
きく、 かつ大幅な伸びが確認できる （図表5） 。 世界的なAIブームを受けたデータセンター関連投資の
活発化が背景とみられる。半導体製造設備やデータセンター関連の投資には巨額の資本が必要になる
ため、投資金額を件数で割った一件あたりの投資額が大規模化しやすく、大型投資案件が投資額全体
を押し上げていることがうかがえる。 
次に投資金額が大きかったのは資源・エネルギー安全保障・GX分野だ。具体的には、発電所などの
電力供給設備への投資案件が押し上げている。太陽光発電・洋上風力発電などの再生可能エネルギー
市場で海外企業の参入が増えた結果とみられ、脱炭素化を志向するグローバルな機運が追い風になっ
図表3 対内直接投資 図表4 国内供給力強化投資

ていると言える。加えて、電力需要の大きいデータセンターの立地を最適化するため、電力源と通信
インフラを一体的に整備する「ワットビット連携」が推進される中で、電力供給設備への投資需要が
高まっていることも当該分野の投資押上げに寄与しているとみられる。 
これらの分野では引き続きグローバルトレンドに沿った投資流入増が見込めそうである一方、造船
分野や防衛産業分野、量子分野、海洋分野の投資額は、増加しているものの依然として小規模だ。例
えば、量子分野は研究開発～実証段階の技術が中心の新領域であり、分野の将来的な成長期待は大き
い。現時点では収益化まで距離があることから、投資額の小ささ自体が問題とは言えないものの、米
国等と比べると日本の産業基盤は相対的に小さく、 スタートアップ企業の集積でも遅れを取っている。
さらに投資を呼び込むうえでは、こうした遅れを埋める手立てを講じることが必要不可欠であろう。 
また、造船分野では、2020年代に入ってから中国の存在感が一段と高まっている。日本の造船会社
による船舶の新規受注量は2000年代半ばから減少しており、世界の船舶建造量に占める日本のシェア
も低下傾向だ。背景には、中国と比べた事業所規模の小ささが指摘されており、生産コストなどの面
で水をあけられている。 
■ 幅広い分野での外国資本取り込みに向けて供給面 ・ 制度面のボトルネック軽減が重要 
戦略分野全体でみれば投資流入の堅調さが確認できた一方で、17の戦略分野それぞれにおいて投資
流入増を実現していく1ためには、 投資の門戸を開くだけでは不十分だ。 造船分野や量子分野に関して
指摘したような課題の解決に向けては、ボトルネックを軽減する地道な取り組みが必要であろう。ま
た、足元で急速に拡大しているデータセンター投資や再エネ関連投資についても、今後は成長の維持
が課題となる。 
外国資本取り込みにおける具体的なボトルネックとして、人材不足等の供給制約や複雑な制度、言
語障壁が挙げられる。日本国内の外資系企業を対象にした、日本貿易振興機構による「2025年度外資
系企業ビジネス実態調査」 では、 直近1～2年の日本のビジネス環境の課題について、 多くの企業が 「高
度人材等の確保」 と 「一般人材の確保」 を回答しているほか、 直近では 「オフィス・事業用地の確保」
図表5 戦略17分野別にみた国内供給力強化投資額の推移

（注） 各社資料で確認できなかった具体的な投資額はfDi Marketsによる推計値。 期間内に国内供給力強化投資が確認できなかった分野につい
ては除外。各社の個別投資プロジェクトの情報及び、日本成長戦略会議資料を基に、みずほ総合研究所調査部が分類

4

を回答する企業の割合も高まっている。こうした人材・立地面等の供給制約は、外資の取り込みを進
めるうえで課題となる。 
また、制度面について、日本貿易振興機構による「ジェトロ対日投資報告2018」では、外国企業が
日本でビジネス展開するうえでの阻害要因として「行政手続きの複雑さ」や「許認可制度の厳しさ」
等が挙げられ、外国企業にとって日本のビジネス環境への適応が容易ではないことがうかがえる。な
お、 同調査では 「外国語によるコミュニケーションの難しさ」 が2位にランクするなど、 言語障壁も外
国企業の参入を阻む一因だ。 
供給面・制度面のボトルネック解消に向けた対応として、人材不足については、大学等の研究・教
育機関との連携強化を通じた国内人材の育成・確保や、高度外国人材の受入強化による対応が考えら
れる。また、用地不足に対しては、産業用地の確保や空き家・空き地の有効活用を通じて、外国企業
が拠点を設立しやすい環境を整備することが重要だろう。 
制度面の対応としては、まず、手続きの一本化が挙げられる。現状、外国企業等の投資申請に際し
ては担当省庁・公的機関と個別にやり取りするケースがあり、外国企業の手続き負担は小さいとは言
えない。 この点、OECD（2026） は外国企業 ・ 投資家と関係省庁等との調整を担う 「ワンストップ窓口」
の機能強化を提言している。 言語障壁については近年通訳や英訳による手続き支援が進みつつあるが、
主要な政策等については英語アナウンスを適時提供するなど、言語障壁軽減に向けたさらなる取り組
みが求められる。

［参考文献］ 
OECD (2022), FDI Qualities Policy Toolkit, OECD Publishing 
OECD (2025), OECD Benchmark Definition of Foreign Direct Investment,  5th edition, OECD

や知的財産の流出リスクには留意し、慎重に議論を進める必要がある。

## Document 056: 2026年9月贸易数据点评：进出口双双超预期，出口增势多点发散

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年9月贸易数据点评：进出口双双超预期，出口增势多点发散.pdf
Report source: 西南证券
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

请务必阅读正文后的重要声明部分  
1 
ooo[Table_ReportInfo] 2026 年 10 月 15 日      
证 券研究报告 •宏观简评报告      
数据点评  
进出口双双超预期，出口增势多点发散  
——2026 年 9 月贸易数据点评

点评

西 南证券研究院  
[Table_Summary]  摘要：从贸易总体情况看，9 月我国货物贸易出口、 进口同比分别增长36.3%、
31.0%，较上月分别加快 11.3、2.8 个百分点；1-9 月进出口总额同比增长
23.8%，贸易顺差达 9381.1亿美元。展望后续，全球AI 算力投资、汽车及机
电产品出口有望继续提供支撑， 但高基数、海外需求波动及贸易政策不确定性
可能带来扰动。从贸易伙伴看，9 月我国对东盟、韩国、欧盟出口同比分别增
长 52.3%、68.2%、25.3%，较上月分别加快22.1、18.9、18.6个百分点，反
映区域产业链贸易联系深化及 AI 产业链需求增长；对欧出口提速也可能受到
企业提前出货影响。对美贸易增速同步回升， 中美关税减让共识有望改善双边
贸易预期，但具体安排仍待落地。从出口结构看，机电产品、高新技术产品出
口金额同比分别增长43.7%、63.6%，集成电路、自动数据处理设备及零部件
出口分别增长 136.0%、92.9%， 全球AI 算力基础设施建设持续提供需求支撑。
不过，集成电路出口数量仅增长 1.1%，出口金额高增仍较多受到价格因素推
动。 汽车、 家电出口金额分别增长62.0%、31.7%， 出口数量分别增长55.5%、
25.4%，量价共同支撑出口增长；成品油出口金额增长 125.1%，出口动能进
一步扩散。 从进口结构看，集成电路、 自动数据处理设备及零部件进口金额分
别增长 74.7%、296.5%，机床进口金额增长 23.8%，电子产业链和高端制造
相关需求保持较强。 原油进口金额同比增长3.3%， 但进口数量仍下降21.5%，
金额回升主要受到国际油价上涨支撑；铁矿石进口金额、 数量分别下降7.4%、
5.6%，反映传统资源品进口需求偏弱。汽车进口金额和数量分别下降38.1%、
47.2%，国产车型替代效应延续。整体看，9 月外贸增长动能有所扩散，出口
受 AI 产业链、汽车及机电产品共同支撑，进口则呈现高技术产品偏强、部分
资源品及汽车偏弱的结构性分化。 
 外贸表现超预期，进出口双双提速。按美元计，2026 年 1‑ 9 月，我国货物贸
易进出口总额同比增长23.8%， 涨幅相比1‑ 8 月扩大 1.4 个百分点， 持续保持
高位。1‑ 9 月，贸易顺差为 9381.12亿美元，相比 1‑8 月增加 1326.07 亿美
元。按美元计，9 月单月， 货物贸易出口、 进口同比分别增长36.3%、31.0%，
涨幅分别较 8 月回升 11.3 和 2.8 个百分点。9 月出口增长主要受 AI产业链需
求旺盛、 汽车及机电产品出口走强支撑，同时区域产业链贸易联系深化， 对欧
出口也可能存在提前出货现象。进口方面，电子链产品进口保持强势，部分资
源品进口表现分化， 原油进口金额回升则受到国际油价上涨支撑。从景气表现
来看，9 月美欧日制造业景气保持扩张： 标普全球美国制造业PMI 终值为 55.9，
创下自 2022 年 5 月以来的最高水平，产出和新订单以更快的速度扩张；欧元
区制造业 PMI终值上升至 52.9，新订单以自2022年 3月以来最快的速度增长，
出口销售连续第二个月扩大；日本制造业 PMI 终值回落至 54.1，企业记录到
产出和新订单的增长放缓。贸易政策方面，9 月下旬中美就 300 亿美元对等降
税达成框架共识， 但安排尚未落地；降税范围集中于消费品等非敏感品类，高
端制造领域贸易壁垒保持不变。10 月中欧开展贸易磋商，双方就混动汽车达
成贸易谅解，并继续推进电动车反补贴案价格承诺后续程序，有助于降低短期
贸易冲突升级风险。10 月以来，美伊围绕霍尔木兹海峡的紧张局势持续升温，
伊朗加强对海上运输活动限制， 美国持续强化对伊朗石油贸易的封锁，区域供

2
应风险上升。整体看，9月进出口同步提速，出口增长动能相对多元，进口则
更多受到电子产业链需求及大宗商品价格变化影响。预计AI 算力投资、汽车
及机电产品出口将继续提供支撑， 但高基数、 海外需求波动及贸易政策不确定
性可能带来扰动， 部分企业提前出货也可能透支后续需求。进口方面，电子产
业链需求有望延续支撑， 能源及资源品进口则更多取决于国内生产需求和国际
价格变化。
 出口高增覆盖多元贸易伙伴，“抢出口”效应带动对欧出口走强。以美元计，
从贸易总额看，9 月我国与主要贸易伙伴的进出口均有所改善。 对东盟、 欧盟、
美国的进出口总额同比增速分别扩大 13.8、12.0、4.8 个百分点至 45.8%、
16.8%、34.8%。其中，对东盟贸易增速提升幅度较大，反映区域产业链贸易
联系持续深化；对美贸易方面，9 月我国对美进出口总额同比增速有所回升。
中美达成 300 亿美元进口商品关税减让共识， 有望改善后续双边贸易预期， 后
续仍需关注具体降税安排及订单变化。从出口总额看，9 月我国对主要贸易伙

伴出口普遍提速。对东盟、韩国、欧盟的出口同比增速分别扩大22.1、18.9、
18.6 个百分点至 52.3%、68.2%、25.3%。东盟和韩国出口增速显著提升，结
合同期集成电路等电子产品出口高增的表现，全球AI 算力投资和半导体产业
链需求可能是重要支撑。对欧出口增速明显回升，可能受到部分企业提前安排
出货的影响。考虑到欧盟持续讨论限制中国汽车进口，相关贸易政策不确定性
可能促使车企提前交付，带动短期对欧出口增长。从进口总额看，9 月我国对
主要贸易伙伴的进口增速整体变化不大，值得注意的是，对韩国进口同比增速
收窄 13.8 个百分点至 94.3%，但仍处于较高水平。结合韩国半导体出口持续
高增的表现，全球 AI 算力投资带来的芯片需求仍是重要支撑。对韩进口增速
回落可能受到商品结构、价格及基数等因素影响。
 出口增长动能多点扩散，高技术出口景气延续。以美元计，从出口金额看，集
成电路、成品油、自动数据处理设备及零部件、汽车（包括底盘）出口金额同
比分别增长 136.0%、125.1%、92.9%和 62.0%；陶瓷产品、肥料、粮食出口

金额同比分别下降 19.1%、9.2%和 5.3%，其中陶瓷和肥料降幅均有所收窄。
从出口数量看，汽车（包括底盘）、成品油、未锻轧铝及铝材、家用电器出口
数量同比分别增长 55.5%、39.3%、26.1%和 25.4%；此前同比下降的稀土、
肥料、船舶出口数量均转为增长，手机、粮食出口数量则分别下降 12.6%和
7.1%。从出口结构看，9 月机电产品、高新技术产品出口金额同比分别增长
43.7%和 63.6%，占出口总额的比重分别为66.7%和 32.9%，仍是出口增长的
重要支撑。其中，集成电路出口金额同比增长136.0%，出口数量同比仅增长
1.1%， 但已由上月下降转为增长， 出口金额高增仍主要受到价格上涨推动； 自
动数据处理设备及零部件出口金额同比增长92.9%，全球 AI 算力基础设施建
设带来的需求增长仍是重要支撑。从边际变化看， 资源品出口表现分化。 肥料
出口金额同比下降 9.2%，降幅收窄 52.7 个百分点，出口数量同比转为增长
12.7%；成品油出口金额同比增长 125.1%，出口数量同比增长 39.3%，数量

和金额同比涨幅均扩大。劳动密集型产品方面，箱包、鞋靴、陶瓷等产品出口
金额和数量同比表现均有所改善， 但陶瓷出口金额仍下降19.1%。机电产品方
面， 手机和船舶出口金额同比增速分别收窄15.4 和 18.3 个百分点至 14.2%和
2.7%， 但两者出口数量增速均较上月改善。 相比之下， 汽车和家电出口金额同
比增速分别扩大至 62.0%和 31.7%， 出口数量同比增速也分别扩大至55.5%和
25.4%，呈现量价共同支撑的特征。总体而言，9月出口增长由 AI产业链、汽
车、 家电及成品油等多个品类共同支撑， 其中芯片出口金额增长对价格变化较
为敏感，汽车和家电则有较强的实物量支撑。后续来看，外需韧性及 AI 算力
基础设施建设有望继续支撑集成电路、 计算机设备等产品出口，但高基数及海
外需求波动可能对增速形成扰动。
 能源及资源品进口量承压，AI链产品量价现改善。以美元计， 从农产品及能源
进口看，粮食和大豆进口金额同比分别增长6.0%和 2.8%，增速均边际回落；
进口数量同比分别下降2.2%和 7.7%，主要受高基数叠加国内豆粕饲用需求偏

### Page 3

请务必阅读正文后的重要声明部分
3
弱影响。原油进口金额同比由降转升至3.3%，进口数量同比下降 21.5%，降
幅继续收窄。金额与数量走势分化，主要受到国际油价上涨影响， 原油进口实
物量仍处于深度收缩区间。从资源品进口看，9 月铁矿砂及其精矿进口金额、
数量同比分别下降 7.4%和 5.6%，均由升转降，反映黑色产业链相关进口需求
有所走弱。肥料进口金额、数量同比分别增长22.6%和 23.4%，涨幅均较上月
收窄 50 多个百分点，主要是前期进口集中释放之后的正常回调。钢材进口金
额同比增长 4.6%，数量同比下降 10.6%，降幅有所收窄，实物进口量仍然偏
弱。从高技术及机电产品进口看，集成电路进口金额同比增长74.7%，增速收
窄 8.9 个百分点，而进口数量同比仅增长1.7%，金额与数量走势明显分化，
芯片价格上涨及进口产品结构变化是金额高增长的重要原因。 自动数据处理设
备及其零部件进口金额同比增速扩大87.5 个百分点至 296.5%， 反映相关设备
进口金额显著走强。机床进口金额同比由降转升至 23.8%，数量同比增长

7.7%，反映高端制造需求扩张的趋势持续。 汽车进口金额、 数量同比分别下降
38.1%和 47.2%，其中金额降幅扩大、数量降幅收窄，整体进口仍处于明显收
缩区间，反映国产高端新能源车型的替代效应持续显现。整体来看，9 月进口
延续结构性分化， 电子设备及高端制造相关产品进口走强， 能源及部分资源品
进口偏弱，汽车进口持续收缩。展望后续，高技术及高端制造相关产品有望成
为进口增长的重要支撑， 能源及资源品进口仍取决于国内需求与国际价格， 汽
车进口则继续面临国产车型的替代压力。
 风险提示：国内政策落地不及预期，海外经济波动超预期， 地缘政治风险超预
期。

分析师承诺  
本报告署名分析师具有中国证券业协会授予的证券投资咨询执业资格并注册为证券分析师，报告所采用的数据均
来自合法合规渠道，分析逻辑基于分析师的职业理解，通过合理判断得出结论，独立、客观地出具本报告。分析师承
诺不曾因，不因，也将不会因本报告中的具体推荐意见或观点而直接或间接获取任何形式的补偿。

投资评级说明  
报告中投资建议所涉及的评级分为公司评级和行业评级（另有说明的除外）。评级标准为报告发布日后 6 个月内
的相对市场表现，即：以报告发布日后 6 个月内公司股价（或行业指数）相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数的涨跌 幅
作为基准。其中：A 股市场以沪深 300 指数为基准， 新三板市场以三板成指 （针对协议转让标的） 或三板做市指数 （针
对做市转让标的）为基准；香港市场以恒生指数为基准；美国市场以纳斯达克综合指数或标普 500 指数为基准。  
公司评级  
买入：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅在 20%以上  
持有：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅介于 10%与 20%之间 
中性：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅介于 -10%与 10%之间  
回避：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅介于 -20%与-10%之间  
卖出：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅在 -20%以下  
行业评级  
强于大市：未来 6 个月内，行业整体回报高于同期相关证券市场代表性指数 5%以上 
跟随大市：未来 6 个月内，行业整体回报介于同期相关证券市场代表性指数 -5%与 5%之间 
弱于大市：未来 6 个月内，行业整体回报低于同期相关证券市场代表性指数 -5%以下

Earnings revision: We factor in Landmark 3Q26 revenues and revise up earnings by 5%/ 
1%/ 1%/ 1% in 2026-29E mainly on higher revenues of CW laser epi-wafer and diodes 
for 1.6T products, supported by rising demand from high-speed optical modules. We 
keep GM and Opex ratio largely unchanged.

Valuation: We employ a discounted P/E methodology (unchanged) and apply a 61.5x 
(unchanged, report link) target P/E multiple on our 2029E EPS, discounted back to 
2027E at a COE of 10.5% (beta 1.7x, risk free rate 1.6%, and market risk premium at 
5.1% - unchanged). Our target P/E multiple of 61.5x is derived from the PEG&M ratio of 
peers (unchanged) in the photonics supply chain. Consequently, our 12-month target 
price is now NT$4,459 (vs. previously at NT$4,401). Our new TP implied 2028E P/E of 
76x is between company’s avg. P/E at 62x and avg. +1 P/E at 91x. Maintain Buy.

### Page 3

Price Target Risks and Methodology - LandMark Optoelectronics Corp.  
Valuation: We employ a discounted P/E methodology, applying a 61.5x target P/E 
multiple on our 2029E EPS, then discount it back to 2027E at a COE of 10.5% (beta 1.7x, 
risk free rate 1.6% and market risk premium at 5.1%), to derive our 12-month target 
price of NT$4,459. Our target P/E multiple is based on the same PEG&M ratio as peers in 
the photonics supply chain. We are Buy-rated on LandMark. 
Key risk: Slower-than-expected SiPh penetration; stronger-than-expected competition; 
Integrated Device Manufacturers (IDMs) offering Epitaxial (EPI) wafers in-house.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

King Slide Works Co. Ltd.  | Asia Pacific
Preliminary August Profit and
September Monthly Sales (-31%
MoM/+196% YoY)
Sentiment to stay soft in near term but 2027 outlook is
promising.
King Slide announced its preliminary profit for August, per TWSE regulatory
requirements:
• Revenue was NT$6,466mn (+1% MoM/+344% YoY), 33% of our estimate for
3Q26 of NT$19,555mn (+81% QoQ/+348% YoY) and 39% of consensus' NT
$16,755mn (+55% QoQ/+284% YoY).

• Pretax income was NT$4,826mn (+216% YoY; pretax margin at 74.6%), 29%
of our 3Q26 pretax income estimate of NT$16,377mn (margin at 83.7%) and
34% of consensus' NT$14,094mn (margin at 84.1%).
• Net income was NT$3,819mn (+212% YoY; EPS NT$40.07; NPM 59.1%), 30%
of our 3Q26 net income estimate of NT$12,938mn (EPS NT$135.76; NPM
66.2%) and 34% consensus' NT$11,118mn (EPS NT$114.38; NPM 66.4%).
September monthly sales
• September sales of NT$4,464mn (-31% MoM/+196% YoY), driving 3Q26

revenue to NT$17,338mn (+60% QoQ/+297% YoY), were short of our
estimate of NT$19,555mn (+81% QoQ/+348% YoY) and but ahead of the
consensus forecast of NT$16,755mn (+55% QoQ/+284% YoY).
Our view:
• We think the sequential decline in September  revenue was driven by slower
momentum for some projects, which entered the later part of their product
cycles. While sentiment should stay soft in the near term, we think the
outlook into 2027 is still promising as customers start new product cycles.

• We maintain our view that King Slide's competitiveness in the comprehensive
patent portfolio and strong execution are entrenched and accentuated amid
fast generation migration in AI applications.
• Stay OW with PT of NT$15,000 (30x 2027e P/E).

Morgan Stanley Research 3
Valuation Methodology and Risks
King Slide Works Co. Ltd. (2059.TW)
Base case, 30x 2027e P/E. We use price/earnings as our valuation methodology because we
believe it better captures King Slide's earnings growth potential. We believe our target mul-
tiple is justified by an operating profit CAGR of 77% seen for 2025-28, implying a PEG of 0.4
vs. the average of 1.2 over 2010-24, when the stock was trading at 17-18x P/E with an operating

profit CAGR of 14%.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger-than-expected AI capex
n Stronger server demand
n Faster market share gains
Risks to Downside
n Softer AI capex
n Weaker server demand
n Sluggish kitchen furniture demand
n Competition leading to slower share gains.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

## Document 109: 9月通胀点评：四季度通胀增速或走平

Qibing.Zhu@bocichina.com 
证券投资咨询业务证书编号：S1300516090001

9 月通胀点评

四季度通胀增速或走平   
9 月国际大宗商品价格波动对国内通胀的影响依然较大，国际油价、有色金
属价格造成明显上拉， 黄金价格则造成一定拖累；9 月 AI 产品价格上升对通
胀的影响进一步凸显，CPI 中通信工具分项同比增速连续两个月在 10%上方；
我们认为四季度 CPI 和 PPI 同比增速或在当前增速水平保持窄幅波动， 关注
国际局势、厄尔尼诺、生产端年底补库存等因素对通胀的扰动 。 
 9 月 CPI 环比增长 0.2%，同比增长 0.9%，核心 CPI 同比增长 1.0%，服务
价格同比增长 0.8%，消费品价格同比增长 1.0%。从分项同比看，同比增
速较 8 月上升的仅有交通通信，居住、教育文化娱乐和医疗保健增速持
平于 8 月，衣着、食品烟酒、生活用品及服务、其他用品和服务增速均
较 8 月有不同程度下降。 
 从环比看，9 月食品价格环比上涨 0.4%，涨幅与上月相同，低于季节性
水平 0.8 个百分点，影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.06 个百分点，服务价格由上
月上涨 0.1%转为下降 0.2%，对 CPI 环比的影响由上月上拉约 0.04 个百
分点转为下拉约 0.08 个百分点 ， 工业消费品价格影响CPI 环比上涨约 0.22
个百分点。 
 AI 产品价格带动 9 月 CPI 同比增速上行。从同比看，9 月 CPI 同比增长
0.9%，其中，食品价格影响 CPI 同比下降约 0.29 个百分点，汽油价格影
响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.47 个百分点，扣除能源的工业消费品价格影响 CPI
同比上涨约 0.39 个百分点 ， 服务价格影响CPI 同比上涨约 0.40 个百分点 ；
从细项看，猪肉和鲜菜价格合计影响 CPI 同比下降约 0.33 个百分点，牛
肉和羊肉价格合计影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.08 个百分点 ， 鸡蛋价格影响CPI
同比上涨约 0.07 个百分点，黄金饰品价格影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.11 个百
分点，数据存储设备、平板电脑、计算机和移动电话机价格合计影响CPI
同比上涨约 0.23 个百分点。一方面，9 月 CPI 有三个特点与此前是一致
的，一是食品价格的持续拖累，二是国际能源价格和黄金价格的持续扰
动，三是内需的疲弱反映在服务价格上；另一方面，9 月也出现了新的特
点，最为明显的是 AI 产品需求带动的价格上升是 9 月 CPI 同比增速较 8
月上升的重要因素 ， 体现在通信工具价格9 月同比增长 14.3%、 交通通信
项目 9 月同比增长 4.3%，其次是以牛肉和羊肉为代表的食品价格，出现
环比走势强于季节性的趋势。我们认为四季度 CPI 同比增速或仍将维持
相对平稳的走势，但是需要开始关注 AI 产品需求对价格的推动、极端天
气对食品价格的影响、国际局势不确定性对大宗商品价格波动的影响，
以及 PPI 同比增速上升对 CPI 的外溢性影响。 
 9 月 PPI 环比增长 1.2%，同比增长 5.0%，其中生产资料同比增长 6.5%，
生活资料同比下降 0.1%。PPIRM 环比增长 1.2%，同比增长 6.9% 
 关注年底补库存对 PPI 的影响。9 月 PPI 同比增长 5.0%，涨幅明显高于
万得一致预期 4.3%，影响较大的主要是生产资料中采掘业和原材料工业
价格，从行业看主要的上行拉动，一是石油、有色、化工等受输入性因
素影响较大的行业，二是受 AI 需求带动较大的计算机通信行业，三是受
季节性因素影响较大的行业，比如煤炭。我们认为四季度国际局势的不
确定性和国内生产端季节性补库存的具体情况 ， 都可能对PPI 价格造成影
响 ， 但考虑到目前国内供强虚弱的情况没有明显变化 ， 我们认为PPI 同比
增速短期虽然回落空间不大，但进一步上升的空间也较小 。 
 风险提示：全球通胀二次上行的风险 ；欧美经济回落速度 偏快；国际局
势复杂化。

### Page 2

2026 年 10 月 14 日 9 月通胀点评 2

CPI 
9 月 CPI 环比增速较 8 月回落。9 月 CPI 环比增长 0.2%，增速较 8 月下降 0.2 个百分点。从分项环
比看，9 月环比上行的包括交通通信 （1.0%） 、 衣着 （0.6%） 、 食品烟酒 （0.2%） 、 医疗保健 （0.2%），
环比增速下降的有生活用品及服务 （-0.1%） 、 教育文化娱乐 （-0.3%） 、 其他用品和服务 （-0.8%），
居住价格环比增速持平；9 月分项中较 8 月环比增速上升的有衣着、医疗保健、生活用品及服务，
较 8 月环比增速下降的有食品烟酒、教育文化娱乐、交通通信、其他用品和服务，居住环比增速持
平于 8 月。 
9 月 CPI 同比增速略低于万得一致预期。9 月 CPI 同比增长 0.9%，较 8 月上升 0.1 个百分点，核心
CPI 同比增长 1.0%，较8 月持平 ， 服务价格同比增长0.8%，较8 月持平 ， 消费品价格同比增长1.0%，
较 8 月上升 0.2 个百分点。从分项同比看，9 月同比增速增长的是其他用品和服务（5.9%） 、交通通
信（4.3%） 、医疗保健 （2.7%） 、教育文化娱乐 （1.4%） 、衣着 （1.2%） 、生活用品及服务 （0.2%），
同比增速下降的是居住（-0.3%）和食品烟酒（-0.9%）；同比增速较 8 月上升的仅有交通通信，居
住、教育文化娱乐和医疗保健增速持平于 8 月，衣着、食品烟酒、生活用品及服务、其他用品和服
务增速均较 8 月有不同程度下降。 
9 月食品价格分化，牛羊肉价格环比涨幅扩大。从环比看，9 月食品价格环比上涨 0.4%，涨幅与上
月相同，低于季节性水平 0.8 个百分点，影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.06 个百分点；鲜菜、猪肉、鸡蛋和
鲜果价格涨幅在 0.5%-2.3%之间，合计影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.05 个百分点；牛肉和羊肉价格持续上
涨，本月分别上涨 3.1%和 1.4%，涨幅均有扩大，合计影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.04 个百分点；休渔期
结束，水产品供应充足，价格下降 0.8%，影响 CPI 环比下降约 0.02 个百分点。从同比看，食品价格
同比下降 1.7%，降幅比上月扩大 0.3 个百分点，影响 CPI 同比下降约 0.29 个百分点；食品中，猪肉
和鲜菜价格分别下降 10.0%和 8.1%，合计影响 CPI 同比下降约 0.33 个百分点；粮食、食用油、奶类
和鲜果价格降幅在 0.6%-1.5%之间 ， 合计影响CPI 同比下降约 0.07 个百分点 ； 鸡蛋价格上涨17.4%，
涨幅回落 1.1 个百分点 ， 影响CPI 同比上涨约 0.07 个百分点 ； 牛肉和羊肉价格分别上涨 7.5%和 6.4%，
涨幅继续扩大，合计影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.08 个百分点。 
服务价格和黄金价格环比回落 ，AI 产品价格同比涨幅扩大。从环比看 ，9 月服务价格由上月上涨 0.1%
转为下降 0.2%，对CPI 环比的影响由上月上拉约 0.04 个百分点转为下拉约 0.08 个百分点 ， 服务中，
随着暑假结束，飞机票、宾馆住宿和旅行社收费价格合计影响 CPI 环比下降约 0.12 个百分点；秋季
开学，教育服务价格影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.03 个百分点；工业消费品价格影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.22
个百分点，其中，受国际市场价格变动影响，国内汽油价格和黄金饰品价格两项合计影响 CPI 环比
上涨约 0.14 个百分点；受成本走高影响，移动电话机、计算机、数据存储设备和平板电脑价格合计
影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.04 个百分点 ； 秋装上市 ， 带动服装价格影响CPI 环比上涨约 0.03 个百分点。
从同比看，9 月汽油价格影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.47 个百分点；扣除能源的工业消费品价格影响 CPI
同比上涨约 0.39 个百分点 ； 其中 ， 黄金饰品价格影响CPI 同比上涨约 0.11 个百分点 ， 数据存储设备、
平板电脑、计算机和移动电话机价格合计影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.23 个百分点；服务价格涨幅与上月
相同，影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.40 个百分点。 
AI 产品价格带动 9 月 CPI 同比增速上行。从同比看，9 月 CPI 同比增长 0.9%，其中，食品价格影
响 CPI 同比下降约 0.29 个百分点 ， 汽油价格影响CPI 同比上涨约 0.47 个百分点 ， 扣除能源的工业消
费品价格影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.39 个百分点 ， 服务价格影响CPI 同比上涨约 0.40 个百分点 ； 从细项
看，猪肉和鲜菜价格合计影响 CPI 同比下降约 0.33 个百分点，牛肉和羊肉价格合计影响 CPI 同比上
涨约 0.08 个百分点 ， 鸡蛋价格影响CPI 同比上涨约 0.07 个百分点 ， 黄金饰品价格影响CPI 同比上涨
约 0.11 个百分点 ， 数据存储设备 、 平板电脑 、 计算机和移动电话机价格合计影响CPI 同比上涨约 0.23
个百分点。一方面，9 月 CPI 有三个特点与此前是一致的，一是食品价格的持续拖累，二是国际能
源价格和黄金价格的持续扰动，三是内需的疲弱反映在服务价格上；另一方面， 9 月也出现了新的
特点 ， 最为明显的是AI 产品需求带动的价格上升是 9 月 CPI 同比增速较 8 月上升的重要因素 ， 体现
在通信工具价格 9 月同比增长 14.3%、交通通信项目 9 月同比增长 4.3%，其次是以牛肉和羊肉为代
表的食品价格，出现环比走势强于季节性的趋势。我们认为四季度 CPI 同比增速或仍将维持相对平
稳的走势，但是需要开始关注 AI 产品需求对价格的推动、极端天气对食品价格的影响、国际局势不
确定性对大宗商品价格波动的影响，以及 PPI 同比增速上升对 CPI 的外溢性影响。

### Page 4

2026 年 10 月 14 日 9 月通胀点评 4

PPI 
PPI 环比大幅走高。9 月 PPI 环比增长 1.2%，较8 月上升 0.8 个百分点 ， 其中生产资料环比增长1.4%，
生活资料环比增长 0.1%。9 月 PPI 同比增长 5.0%，较 8 月上升 1.2 个百分点，其中生产资料同比增
长 6.5%，较 8 月上升 1.5 个百分点，生活资料同比下降 0.1%，较 8 月上升 0.4 个百分点。 
9 月 PPIRM 环比增长 1.2%，同比增长 6.9%，较 8 月上升 1.1 个百分点；从构成来看，9 月同比增速
上升较多的是有色金属材料类（ 20.0%），燃料、动力类（ 13.6%）和化工原料类（ 12.6%），同比
增速下降的仅有建筑材料类（-2.3%），农副产品类（-0.4%）和黑色金属材料类（-0.4%）；所有品
类中，同比增速较 8 月上升较多的是燃料、动力类，化工原料类和建筑材料类，同比增速下降的仅
有黑色金属材料类。 
9 月主要行业中出厂价格同比增速较高的是，煤炭开采和洗选业（ 35.3%），石油和天然气开采业
（24.5%）和石油、煤炭及其他燃料加工业（21.6%），同比增速较低的是酒、饮料和精制茶制造业
（-5.3%），电力、热力的生产和供应业（-3.9%）和黑色金属矿采选业（-3.4%）；同比增速较 8 月
上升较多的是石油和天然气开采业，石油、煤炭及其他燃料加工业和煤炭开采和洗选业，下降的仅
有黑色金属矿采选业，造纸及纸制品业，电力、热力的生产和供应业以及木材加工及木、竹、藤、
棕、草制品业。 
输入性因素和季节性因素对 PPI 同比增速影响较大。9 月 PPI 环比运行的主要特点 ：一是国际原油、
有色金属价格上行带动国内相关行业价格上涨 。 石油开采 、 精炼石油产品制造 、 有机化学原料制造、
有色金属矿采选业 、 有色金属冶炼和压延加工业 ，5 个行业合计影响 PPI 环比上涨约 0.63 个百分点。
二是部分能源和原材料行业供需状况变化拉动价格上涨。受冬季供暖备煤需求增加、主产矿区供应
阶段性偏紧、国际煤价上涨预期效应等因素影响，煤炭开采和洗选业、煤炭加工价格环比上涨，钢
材、水泥等建筑材料需求有所回升，加之原材料成本上涨，非金属矿物制品业价格在环比连降 7 个
月后转为上涨，黑色金属冶炼和压延加工业价格环比上涨，4 个行业合计影响 PPI 环比上涨约 0.38
个百分点。三是人工智能、算电协同等领域加快发展带动部分行业 需求增加、价格上涨。电气机械
和器材制造业 、 计算机通信和其他电子设备制造业2 个行业合计影响 PPI 环比上涨约 0.07 个百分点。
从同比看，价格上涨的行业中，煤炭开采和洗选业、石油和天然气开采业、石油煤炭及其他燃料加
工业、化学原料和化学制品制造业、有色金属矿采选业、有色金属冶炼和压延加工业、电气机械和
器材制造业、计算机通信和其他电子设备制造业，8 个行业涨幅比上月均扩大，合计影响 PPI 同比
上涨约 5.42 个百分点，上拉影响比上月增加 1.12 个百分点，价格下降的行业中，酒饮料和精制茶制
造业、电力热力生产和供应业、医药制造 业、非金属矿物制品业、汽车制造业， 5 个行业合计下拉
PPI 同比约 0.66 个百分点。 
关注年底补库存对 PPI 的影响。9 月 PPI 同比增长 5.0%，涨幅明显高于万得一致预期 4.3%，影响较
大的主要是生产资料中采掘业和原材料工业价格，从行业看主要 的上行拉动，一是石油、有色、化
工等受输入性因素影响较大的行业 ，二是受AI 需求带动较大的计算机通信行业 ， 三是受季节性因素
影响较大的行业，比如煤炭。我们认为四季度国际局势的不确定性和国内生产端季节性补库存的具
体情况，都可能对 PPI 价格造成影响，但考虑到目前国内供强虚弱的情况没有明显变化，我们认为
PPI 同比增速短期虽然回落空间不大，但进一步上升的空间也较小 。

本报告准确表述了证券分析师的个人观点。该证券分析师声明，本人未在公司内、外部机构兼任有损本人独立性与客
观性的其他职务，没有担任本报告评论的上市公司的董事、监事或高级管理人员；也不拥有与该上市公司有关的任何
财务权益；本报告评论的上市公司或其它第三方都没有或没有承诺向本人提供与本报告有关的任何补偿或其它利益。  
中银国际证券股份有限公司同时声明，将通过公司网站披露本公司授权公众媒体及其他机构刊载或者转发证券研究报
告有关情况 。 如有投资者于未经授权的公众媒体看到或从其他机构获得本研究报告的 ， 请慎重使用所获得的研究报告，
以防止被误导，中银国际证券股份有限公司不对其报告理解和使用承担任何责任。  
评级体系说明 
以报告发布日后公司股价/行业指数涨跌幅相对同期相关市场指数的涨跌幅的表现为基准： 
公司投资评级： 
买    入：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 20%以上； 
增    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 10%-20%； 
中    性：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数变动幅度在-10%-10%之间； 
减    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数跌幅在 10%以上； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

行业投资评级： 
强于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现强于基准指数； 
中    性：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现基本与基准指数持平； 
弱于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现弱于基准指数； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

沪深市场基准指数为沪深 300 指数；新三板市场基准指数为三板成指或三板做市指数；香港市场基准指数为恒生指数
或恒生中国企业指数；美股市场基准指数为纳斯达 克综合指数或标普 500 指数。

## Document 117: [000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 소음보다 방향성에SK하이닉스(000660) 소음보다 방향성에소음보다 방향성에 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 소음보다 방향성에SK하이닉스(000660) 소음보다 방향성에소음보다 방향성에 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-09-28
Pages: 5

### Page 1

기업분석 2025.09.28 www.daishin.com

Issue & News 
SK하이닉스  
(000660) 
 소음보다 방향성에 
류형근

hyungkeun.ryu@daishin.com

투자의견 BUY  
매수, 유지 
6개월 목표주가 3,200,000 
유지  
현재주가 
(26.09.23) 
1,862,000 
반도체업종

◼ 최근 시장은 2가지 Risk를 제기. 다만, 과도한 우려는 기우. 
◼ 현 주가는 보다 강인해지는 Fundamental의 가치를 미반영한 수준. 
◼ 비중 확대 전략 유효. 목표주가와 매수의견 유지.

투자전략: 업황을 대변하지 못하고 있는 주가 
목표주가와 매수의견 유지. 현 주가는 업황의 강세를 충분히 반영하지 못하
고 있다고 생각. 1) 메모리반도체 사이클의 추가 확장 (구매 경쟁 심화에 따른 
단기 가격 상승 폭 확대, 기대치를 상회할 4Q26 DRAM 판매량), 2) 3Q26 실
적발표에서 보다 구체화될 주주환원 정책의 방향성에 보다 집중할 필요. 
시장에서 제기하는 Risk가 있다면, 2가지. 1) 주요 고객사향 HBM4 품질 이슈, 
2) Solidigm IPO와 해외 공장 투자에 따른 경계심리 확대가 그 대상. 당사는 
해당 Risk가 Fundamental을 저해할 것으로 보고 있지 않으며, 단기 노이즈보
다는 사이클 확장의 선순환에 보다 주목할 필요가 있다고 생각. 
1. HBM4 품질 이슈, 과도한 우려는 기우 
3Q26부터 HBM4 출하가 본격화될 것이라는 기존 입장을 유지. HBM4 비중
은 3Q26 전체 판매량의 40%, 2027년 연간 50% 중반까지 상승할 것. 
일각에서는 주요 고객사향 HBM4 품질 이슈로 사업 성과가 기대치를 하회할 
수 있다는 우려를 표출. 다만, 과도한 우려는 기우. 후공정 단에서 문제가 일
부 발생했을 수 있으나, 해결 불가능한 Risk라 보기 어렵고, 불량에 따른 배
상금을 논할 단계도 아니라는 판단. 
2. 해외 공장 투자, 실보다 득이 크다 
언론보도에 따르면, SK하이닉스는 해외 공장 투자와 Solidigm IPO를 추진 중. 
해당 결정이 현실화될 경우, 시장은 단기적으로 이를 부정적으로 받아들일 
가능성이 존재. 다만, 결과적으로, 실보다 득이 큰 선택이 될 것이라 생각. 
- 해외 투자가 현실화되더라도, 실질 생산 기여 시점은 2028년 이후일 가능
성이 높음. 미국의 건설 경기와 공장 구축의 세부 단계 등을 감안할 필요. 
- 해외 투자 결정이 관세 Risk를 축소시킬 수 있는 변화라는 점도 감안할 필
요. “불확실성 해소”는 Upcycle의 중반부에서 반도체 주식에 대한 좋은 투자
포인트가 되어왔고, 이번에도 다르지 않을 것이라는 판단.

## Document 134: [329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

HD현대중공업의 3Q26 연결 실적은 매출액 5조 8,602억원(yoy +33%, qoq -7%), 
영업이익 9,161억원(yoy +64%, qoq -12%, OPM 15.6%)으로 시장 컨센서스를 하회할 
전망이다. 동사의 3Q26 조업일수는 여름휴가, 추석연휴 및 중대재해 등 영향으로 
qoq -21% 감소했으며 환율 역시 qoq -84원 감소한 영향을 감안해 전분기 대비 
실적을 보수적으로 추정했다. 그럼에도 해양 부문 Trion FPU발 C/O에 따른 일회성 
환입이 예상됨에 따라 일부 이익 상쇄가 가능할 것으로 예상된다. 한편, 동사의 
임단협 부결로 당분기 추가적인 인건비 부담은 제한적일 것으로 판단되며 잠수함 
화재 사고 관련 비용 역시 4Q 이후 반영될 전망이다.

동사는 급격히 커져가는 AIDC향 전력수요 및 발전용 엔진 수주에 대응하고자 9/10 
총 4GW 규모의 4행정 중속엔진 캐파 증설을 위한 신규 투자를 공시헸다. 동사의 
기존 DC향 4행정 중속엔진 수주 단가를 고려 시 GW당 약 1조원의 매출 창출이 
가능할 전망이며 2행정 및 선박용 4행정 엔진 대비 높은 수익성이 예상되는 바 
생산이 본격화될 28년부터 동사 실적 성장성의 핵심 동인으로 작용할 전망이다.

동사에 대해 투자의견 BUY를 유지하며 업종 밸류에이션 디레이팅 및 시장 프리미엄 
조정에 따른 Target Multipe 변동으로 목표주가를 70만원으로 하향 조정한다. 상선 
시장 수주 경쟁 심화로 업종 밸류 조정이 이루어지고 있으나 동사는 엔진 기반 장기 
성장성으로 Peer 대비 상대적 매력도 확보 및 주가 방어가 가능하다는 판단이다.

## Document 169: August CPI – Came in on our expectations (2026-10-18)

Source PDF: all report pdf/August CPI – Came in on our expectations (2026-10-18).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 16
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#inflation #australia #rba #economicbulletinsaustralia #inflation
August CPI – Came in on our expectations
By Justin Smirk & Neha Sharma
15:27 September 30 2026 Share
August Monthly CPI: Headline 0.4%mth/4.0%yr, Trimmed Mean 0.2%mth/3.6%yr. Market
services ex volatile –0.4%mth/3.3%yr.

The CPI rose 0.4% in August, spot on Westpac’s expectation but a bit softer than market
expectations of 0.5%. Due to base effects the annual pace picked up to 4.0%yr from 3.5%yr.

### Page 2

Key factors driving the headline result were auto fuels (14.8%); games, toys & hobbies (10.2%) with
rents providing a solid base (0.3%) while some offset was provided by both domestic (–4.9%) and
international holiday travel & accommodation (–1.5%).
Trimmed mean (TM) inflation printed 0.2% again bang on Westpac’s expectation while the market
was slightly higher at 0.3%. The annual pace was flat at 3.6%yr, the third month in a row at this pace,
while the six-month annualise pace was steady at 3.9%yr.

Key factors driving the TM print were: rents (0.3%) and new dwelling purchases (0.2%). The TM
distribution ranged from –0.38% to 0.82%.
Today’s inflation update revealed price discounting for some durable goods as retail demand softens.
We would expect to see a bounce from these categories in the following month. There was also
offsetting strength elsewhere in durable good so at least for now, the momentum continues to
suggest some near term upside risk to our current inflation estimates.

Nevertheless, as the TM came in on expectations and based on a first quick pass at updating the
quarterly profile, we see little pressure to revise our current Q3 estimates of 1.3%qtr for the CPI and
0.9%qtr for the TM.
The RBA remains alert to near term inflationary risks. We are expecting the momentum in TM
inflation to remain well above the top of the target band through to year-end underpinning our
expectations for the RBA to raise rates again in November.

### Page 3

August CPI in more detail
Following the bumper read in July, the monthly CPI moderated in August rising 0.4%mth. This was in
line with our expectations and slightly softer than the market consensus. This took the year-ended pace
to 4.0%yr. On a seasonally adjusted basis the CPI rose 0.7%mth highlighting the impact of seasonal
sales in August.

They key drivers behind the monthly lift were automotive fuel, up 14.8%mth, contributing 0.5ppts to the
headline figure. The surge resulted from the ending of the fuel excise discount on August 3 and rising
global energy prices. With crude oil prices still elevated due to the conflict in the middle east, and
growing shortages of refined products due to both the middle east conflict and Ukrainian assaults on
Russian refining capacity, auto fuel is expected to remain an inflationary impulse at least until the end
of 2026.

The monthly TM also softened, rising 0.2%mth compared to the 0.5%mth read from July. The result
was again in line with our expectations, and very close at two decimal places (0.24%mth vs
0.23%mth), but softer than the market's view. The year-ended pace held at 3.6%yr for the third
consecutive month, although it continued to ease marginally at the second decimal place. In contrast,
the six-month annualised pace has continued to trend higher since April and is currently running at
3.9%yr.

### Page 4

There is a possibility that as the ABS continues to grapple with seasonal adjustment issues we will see
revisions to the TM estimates. Last July also saw a strong 0.48%mth read which eased to 0.29%mth in
August. This is not too dissimilar from the 0.48%mth and 0.24%mth reads for 2026.

We are expecting core inflation to start to moderate as we get to year end but our current estimate for
the month of December still has the TM running at a 3.3% in the year while the six-month annualised
pace is still a chunky 3.1%. Applying a trim on the seasonally adjusted 3m/3m expenditure classes
yields a TM of 0.9%qtr currently. This is in line with our current Q3 TM estimate.

Other measures of underlying inflation told a similar story, easing on a monthly basis but holding steady
on a year-ended basis. Market services ex volatiles fell –0.4%mth to be 3.3%yr higher. Excluding both
volatiles & holidays saw a 0.2%mth/4.1%yr lift.

Most market services components eased in the month (including on a seasonally adjusted basis) but
annual growth across this group as a whole remained above the RBA's 2-3% target band. The near-
term momentum (three-month annualised) is also quite strong across several components of services.
Of note include deposit and loan facilities (11.2%mth), sports participation (10.9%), clothing repair
(7.0%), restaurant meals (6.8%) and other household services (5.2%). We are closely watching market
services as this is where inflationary risks, outside of energy, appear to be most persistent.

Due to its sizable weight in the CPI, and the fact price changes here tend to be less volatile than many
other categories, so they almost always appear in the TM estimates, housing prices remain a key
focus. Rents rose 0.3%mth in August, in line with our expectations, to be up 3.6%yr. Since April, the
seasonally adjusted rents series has been running lower than the non-seasonally adjusted series. We
are expecting rents to hold around this pace till year end before moderating modestly as we move
through 2027.

New dwellings inflation was 0.2%mth in August (softer than our expectation for a 0.4%mth rise). Since
peaking in May, monthly price growth has slowed in each successive month, potentially reflecting
increasing difficulty for developers in passing through higher costs amid softer conditions in established
housing markets and weaker demand in the face of higher interest rates. The annual pace looks to
have topped out. It was at 5.8%yr in June easing to 5.4%yr currently with the three-month annualised
pace at 3.7%. We expect dwelling price inflation to continue easing through to the end of 2027 as
falling established house prices limit the ability of builders to pass on rising construction costs. There is
a medium-term risk that as conditions improve builders may raise prices more aggressively to claw
back margins.

### Page 5

Overall, the breadth of inflation moderated in August. The share of the basket running above 3%yr
eased from 45% to 42%, with the share below 2%yr increasing from 38% to 41%.

### Page 6

Key surprises in the month:
Upside surprises

Downside surprises
Insurance surprised to the upside (0.7%mth vs 0.3%mth) after a run of weaker-than-usual reads over
the past two months.
Motor vehicle repair costs rose a solid 0.8% against our 0.3%mth expectations, with spare parts and
accessories also up 0.3%mth vs –0.4%mth.
Footwear rose 1.7%mth against our expectations for a -2.9%mth decline, the gains were broad-
based across women, men & children.
Household goods surprised to the upside. Household appliances, utensils and tools rose 1.9%mth
against our flat expectations, meanwhile household textiles fell –1.0%mth against our expectations
for a –5.0%mth fall. Furniture and furnishings also fell by less than expected –0.6%mth vs –1.3%mth.
Recreation and culture surprised to the upside (–1.0%mth vs –1.5%mth), owing to a 1.4%mth lift in
other recreation, sport & culture against our expectations for a flat read on the back of an outsized
gain in games, toys & hobbies (10.2%mth vs 2.5%mth). Holiday travel was marginally less weak than
expected (–3.3% vs –3.5%mth).

### Page 7

Food was the key downside miss falling –0.1%mth against our expectations for a 0.2%mth lift. Meat
& seafood (–0.9%mth vs 0.5%mth) and fruit & vegetables (–1.2%mth vs 0.1%mth) were the key
areas of weakness.
Audio, visual and computing equipment and services came in weaker-than-expected (0.7%mth vs
1.2%mth), owing to a softer 0.6%mth lift in computing services (3.0%mth expected). This was partly
balanced by a stronger than expected lift in computing equipment (0.9%mth vs –0.5%mth). The latter

is where we are most likely to see the impact of the AI boom in the CPI, due to computer chip
inflation, other than construction cost inflation for new dwellings.
Motor vehicles came in lower at –0.4%mth vs –0.1%mth which could be the start of price pressures
associated with the rapid expansion of the share of Chinese cars in the Australian market.
Automotive fuel was also slightly softer at 14.8%mth vs 16.7%mth.
Garments and accessories were both softer. Garments fell –1.9%mth against our expectations for a

0.2%mth rise, meanwhile accessories came in at 0.1%mth vs 0.5%mth expected.

### Page 8

What does this mean for the RBA?
Yesterday, post the RBA rate hike and monetary policy decision statement, Westpac changed its view
from a November follow up rate hike being a risk to being the base case. The post-meeting statement
was notable for its emphasis on the Middle East conflict, noting that “global energy prices are now
much higher than had been assumed in the August forecasts”. In addition, RBA’s liaison suggests firms
are passing this on or planning to do so. The pass-through was characterised as partial, and additional

to domestically driven inflation arising from capacity pressures. We read this as implying that the RBA
is concerned about upside risks to underlying inflation in Q4 of this year, as well as Q3.

### Page 9

Related articles
Our current forecast of 0.9%qtr for the September quarter TM stands post today’s release and
represents a step up from 0.8% in the June quarter holding the annual pace at 3.6%yr. Our current
forecast for the Q4 TM is 0.7%qtr/3.3%yr but given the move in energy prices, and anecdotes of firms
now passing on those rising costs, we too see upside risks to this estimate.

As such, we hold to the view that the RBA will follow up with another rate hike in November. For more
information on the RBA decision please see Luci Ellis’s note “RBA hikes in September, November
follow-up now expected".

## Document 199: Cliff Notes_ getting the balance right (2026-10-19)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ getting the balance right (2026-10-19).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-19
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: getting the balance right
By Ryan Wells, Illiana Jain & Elliot Clarke
10:46 October 16 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
Australia’s key data release this week was the September Labour Force Survey. Employment surprised
to the downside, -39.3k. However, a 0.2ppt decline in labour force participation from August’s near-
historic high largely offset. The unemployment rate therefore held at 4.7% after August was revised up.

Residual seasonality may be in the mix. Regardless, the take home point from the update is that the

### Page 2

labour market is continuing to ease as employment growth runs below working-age population growth.
We expect the unemployment rate to grind higher into 2027 as tighter financial conditions and cost-of-
living pressures restrict activity growth to a below-trend pace. Although, our forecast peak is near at
4.8%, due by Q2 2027.
This update on the labour market complicates the communication challenge for the RBA. The
September minutes were firmly on the hawkish side, with the discussion focussing on materialising

upside risks to inflation. But, with the unemployment rate now through the RBA’s NAIRU assumption of
4.6%, Governor Bullock’s “a little tight” descriptor for the labour market is becoming harder to defend.
Still, with a clear focus on inflation risks – which also led us to revise up our near-term CPI forecasts in
the October Market Outlook – we still expect a 25bp rate hike at the November meeting.
Over in the US, the headline CPI rose 0.6% as expected in September, taking the annual rate up to

3.7%yr. Energy prices were a key contributor (5.2%, 20.4%yr), while core inflation was a touch above
consensus in the month at 0.3% (2.6%yr). Within the core components, core goods prices gained 0.3%
after being broadly unchanged over the past 6 months (0.7%yr). Core services meanwhile rose 0.4%
(3.1%yr) on a 0.4% increase in shelter (3.2%yr), a 0.6% gain for transport services and a 0.3%
increase in medical care services (2.7%yr and 2.5%yr respectively). Further, the subsequent release of

the PPI pointed to continued upstream price pressures, with the headline and core measures up
6.3%yr and 5.0%yr respectively. The latest Beige Book provided colour on these developments, firms
across multiple districts citing tariffs, insurance and commodity prices as ongoing sources of cost
pressure. Guiding on the risks, many respondents expect further price increases as contracts/hedges
roll off.
Given their telegraphed concerns over inflation, there is enough signal in these reports to warrant a

follow-up hike at the October FOMC meeting. Though it seems Committee members believe they are
near the end of the tightening cycle and so have time on their side. This week, Daly and Bowman both
showed patience in assessing the trend, with no clear bias towards an October or December decision.
Williams stated he believes one more hike would prove sufficient in this cycle given known information.
And Waller also showed confidence, highlighting that elevated long-term yields are doing some of the

FOMC’s work, restricting demand in interest rate sensitive sectors. We hold a similar view, anticipating
that one more hike by year end and a little time will bring inflation near the 2.0%yr medium-term target.
However, as discussed in this month’s Market Outlook, capacity pressures are likely to see inflation
consistently above target, rather than below, in coming years – particularly if, as we suspect, capacity
in the housing sector remains tight. This will mean the FOMC have to remain modestly restrictive over

the forecast horizon and will also bias up long-term yields.
In Asia, there was one release that stood out, China’s trade account. At USD133.5bn, the September
result was a whisker inside January 2025’s record high of USD137.9bn. More impressively, throughout
2025 and 2026 year-to-date the surplus has now averaged USD100.9bn, almost 3 times the average of
2019, prior to the pandemic. The geographic breadth of export growth is as impressive as the headline

annual gain of 36.3%yr, even more so given the ongoing strength in Chinese investment in
neighbouring nations’ production chains. In time, both trade and income receipts from offshore
investment should buoy the renminbi, although for now continued outbound foreign investment should
moderate the pace of gains against other nations in the region, and likely versus the US dollar.

### Page 3

Related articles
Another key trend assessed in October’s Market Outlook is the evolution of producer prices across
Asia, and the likelihood of the disinflationary benefit the west has received in recent decades
continuing. Historically, labour cost arbitrage was one of the key drivers of declining goods prices.
While still possible, we expect the next phase of manufacturing development to instead be driven by
capital, technology and energy. Many end users and the manufacturers undertaking production are

also increasingly prioritising resilience, security and specialisation over cost minimisation. It is therefore
more probably future supply-chain developments will help contain rising costs through productivity than
drive sustained price declines for the goods bought by consumers and businesses.

### Page 1

Taiwan Industrials

Investment case: We initiate coverage of Acter, an established 
multinational cleanroom engineering, procurement, construction and 
management (EPCM) turnkey service provider, with a Buy (1) rating. Its 
core business includes cleanroom electromechanical engineering, 
water/gas/chemical delivery systems, and other cleanroom services. We 
believe now is a good time to revisit the stock as it underperformed the 
TAIEX Index by 32.7% in 3Q26, mainly on weaker-than-expected 2Q26 
results and soft sector sentiment. Given its stronger revenue momentum 
over recent months and upcoming TSMC results on 15 October, which may 
drive sentiment, we suggest investors to buy the stock. We forecast a 
24.6% revenue CAGR and 36.9% net profit CAGR over 2026-28E.

Intact global demand for cleanroom technology, the edge AI market, 
and rapid cleanroom expansion in Asia further support Acter’s order 
backlog. Its backlog reached TWD66bn in July 2026 vs. TWD46bn in 2025 
and TWD30bn in 2022 (15.3% 2022-25 CAGR), which largely outpaced the 
global cleanroom technology market’s forecast CAGR over 2023-2032E, 
according to Precedence Research.

Strong competitive edge supports order share gains across multiple 
segments. Acter’s competitive edge lies in 1) intelligent management 
enabled by digital twins and AI, using 3D building information modelling 
(BIM) and AI-driven digital twin management; 2) intellectualisation of green 
engineering, which comprises green design, green procurement, and green 
techniques; 3) experience across multiple industries (ie, data centres and 
electromechanical engineering for the consumer industry) which supports 
better integration; and 4) a complete network, enabling on-the-spot support. 
Acter believes it has the highest market share among the operators of three 
major US data centre players due largely to its capabilities in liquid cooling 
and its expertise in electromechanical and cleanroom engineering.

Catalysts: Near-term: project wins from key clients, supporting stronger 
monthly revenue momentum; mid- to long-term: an improving operating-
margin trend and a resilient order backlog.

Valuation: We are the first non-domestic broker to cover Acter. We initiate 
coverage with a 12-month TP of TWD1,415, based on a target PER of 20x 
(equivalent to a 0.54x PEG over 2026-28E). We believe our valuation is 
fair, given Acter’s strong earnings visibility, which we believe warrants a 
rerating. Our TP is supported by a DCF valuation of TWD1,454.

Risks: 1) weaker-than-expected expansion in the semiconductor industry; 
and 2) project delays or cancellations.

How do we justify our view? 
Growth outlook Valuation Earnings revisions

Growth outlook

Acter: net profit and net-profit growth    
We forecast Acter to record a 36.9% net profit CAGR over 
2026-28E, on the back of a 24.6% revenue CAGR over the 
same period, driven by 1) larger project scale, as strong 
demand for cleanroom EPCM services from the high-tech 
sector is likely to support continued growth in Acter’s order 
backlog and 2) improving gross margins and operating 
efficiency. We expect operating margin to expand by 2.0pp 
in 2027E and 1.3pp in 2028E, driven by favourable gross 
margins on new projects and operating leverage.

Source: Company, Daiwa forecasts

Valuation   Acter: 1-year-forward PER bands    
Our 12-month TP of TWD1,415 is based on a target PER 
of 20x, applied to our 1-year-forward EPS forecast. Our 
target multiple of 20x is higher than the stock’s past-3-year 
1-year-forward PER (12.2x) and past-5-year 1-year-
forward PER (9.6x) and implies a PEG of 0.54x on our 
36.9% net profit CAGR forecast over 2026-28E. We 
believe the stock deserves a rerating in the next 12 
months, largely supported by resilient cleanroom demand 
globally.

Our TP is also supported by an alternative DCF-derived 
valuation of TWD1,454/share.

Source: Bloomberg, Daiwa forecasts

Earnings revisions

Acter: Bloomberg consensus EPS forecast revisions   
Bloomberg consensus EPS estimates for 2026E and 
2027E have risen 24.8% and 24.3% YTD, respectively. 
This is likely due to upward revisions to order backlog and 
earnings forecasts following better-than-expected industry 
outlook. We are the first foreign broker to cover Acter.

Company profile 
Established in 1979, Acter Group (Acter) is a leading engineering services provider specialising in 
cleanroom, electromechanical, and high-tech facility integration solutions for semiconductor, 
optoelectronics, biotech, green energy, and advanced manufacturing clients across Taiwan, China and 
the rest of the world. As of 2025, its revenue comprised cleanroom electromechanical (71%), water, gas 
and chemical delivery systems (20%), consumer industry electromechanical (4%), biomedical (2%) and 
high-tech equipment & materials sales & services (3%).

### Page 5

5

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
Acting on the demand for cleanrooms 
Why are cleanrooms a bottleneck in the high-tech 
industry? 
In recent years, cleanrooms have become a critical bottleneck for high-tech industries, 
especially semiconductor manufacturing, OSAT, AI hardware, and PCB, mainly because of 
its indispensable nature, severe supply-demand imbalance, labour shortages, and high 
technical and regulatory barriers.

Cleanrooms are an indispensable core infrastructure as they determine product 
yield and quality stability. Advanced manufacturing nodes in semiconductors, 
optoelectronics, and biopharmaceuticals require highly particle-free, constant-temperature, 
and constant-humidity environments to ensure reliable production. As process 
technologies continue to advance, such as 2nm node (or even newer nodes) and 
advanced packaging, existing cleanrooms must be rebuilt, and the complexity of building 
new ones thus increases. This keeps overall demand for cleanrooms at high levels.

A severe global supply-demand imbalance with demand for cleanroom construction 
outpacing supply. The surge in AI servers, high-performance computing (HPC), and high-
bandwidth memory (HBM) has triggered a global expansion for front-end wafer fabs, 
packaging and testing facilities, and PCB manufacturing. Order visibility remains extremely 
strong across the industry. According to Markets and Markets, the global cleanroom market 
size is set to expand at a CAGR of 6.6% over 2025-31E. At the same time, the global 
semiconductor cleanroom market size is set to grow at a CAGR of 8.0% over 2025-30E. 
For instance, Acter’s order backlog exceeded TWD66bn at the end of July 2026, up from 
TWD46bn at the end of 2025, showing the urgency of demand for cleanroom construction.

Global: cleanroom market size and growth      Global: semiconductor cleanroom market size and growth

Source: Markets and Markets  Source: Markets and Markets

Human resources are an additional limitation to capacity growth. Cleanroom 
engineering is highly technical and labour-intensive, relying on specialised on-site 
installation and supervision that cannot be replaced by robots. At the same time, the 
industry faces severe labour shortages and an aging workforce, and younger generations 
are reluctant to join field operations. Cross-border staff deployment is also less cost-
efficient than local hiring.

Cleanrooms involve complex system integration, creating high barriers to entry. A 
cleanroom construction includes 4 sequential phases, where each phase usually requires 
totally different areas of expertise. Therefore, the competitive landscape in each area 
differs. The process begins with civil & structural (C&S) works to establish the physical 
plant, followed by mechanical, electrical, and plumbing (MEP) works for essential utility 
generation, where Acter is one of the leading global experts. After MEP is finished, 
chemicals/gas/water are then distributed via main systems through primary piping 
network and finally delivered to production equipment through hook-up systems. As

Cleanrooms are a 
bottleneck in the high-
tech industry, including 
semiconductor foundry, 
memory, and electronic 
assembling

### Page 6

6

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
global supply chains restructure under N+1 (domestic market + 1) strategies, companies 
building overseas facilities in places like the US face strict and complicated licensing 
processes, particularly on fire safety and hazardous chemicals. Without experience dealing 
with such engineering and safety issues, construction timelines can be severely delayed.

As a result, even with advanced equipment and chip designs, a high-tech company cannot 
begin production without adequate cleanroom space, skilled engineers, and system-
integration capabilities. Therefore, cleanroom engineering contractors hold significant 
bargaining power and capacity allocation leverage in today's tech supply chain.

IC foundry 
In the foundry sector, where the cleanroom supply-demand shortage is the tightest, project 
activity remains centred on advanced process capacity expansion in Taiwan and Arizona. 
Simultaneously, domestic advanced node development remains intense, with multi-phase 
fab builds advancing across Hsinchu, Taichung, and Kaohsiung. Local cleanroom and MEP 
integration demand is further boosted by advanced packaging facilities in Taiwan (such as 
Chiayi and Tainan) and upcoming projects in the US are set to contribute revenue for the 
cleanroom providers in the following years.

Memory 
The memory segment is exhibiting robust momentum as well, mainly catalysed by HBM 
capacity additions. Projects such as the plant in Japan, transitioned to contribution for the 
cleanroom EPCM providers starting from 2026. In Taiwan, turnkey engineering contracts in 
Miaoli are also actively underway. This trend is reinforced by stronger-than-expected 
expansion activity from memory producers in China.

OSAT,  PCB and ODM/OEM 
For OSAT companies, demand is also surging due to the spill-over effect of advanced 
packaging protocols and N+1 supply chain restructuring. The OSAT names are expanding 
their footprint both in Southern Taiwan and in overseas locations including in the US and 
Southeast Asia. Major engineering contracts signed between OSAT clients and Acter show 
stable long-term workload for strong cleanroom demand over our forecast horizon. 
Similarly, overseas expansion for PCB manufacturing is peaking in Southeast Asia, 
especially in Thailand. We expect Chinese PCB players to also increase their capacity, just 
like memory companies in the past year. Except for front-end and back-end chip 
manufacturing, ODM/OEM and data centre infrastructure is also a key growth engine for 
the EPCM industry. Major Taiwanese ODMs are building production facilities in Texas and 
Mexico to meet local manufacturing needs. Cleanroom contractors, such as Acter, 
providing MEP and system integration in Texas are directly benefiting from these projects. 
The shift toward liquid cooling systems has also increased engineering complexity, 
favouring few cleanroom companies with the expertise to deliver complex integration for 
CSPs in both North America and Southeast Asia.

Acter: an integrated cleanroom solutions provider  
Acter Group (Acter), an integrated cleanroom solutions provider, maintains its competitive 
positioning within the high-tech cleanroom and MEP engineering sector, driven by its group 
synergy and global footprint. Acter operates as a comprehensive vendor through a group 
business model. The parent company, Acter, supervises overall cleanroom and MEP 
services, while subsidiary Nova Technology (Novatech, 6613 TT, not rated) mainly provides 
chemical primary system and hook-up, and Novatech’s subsidiary Rayzher (7703 TT, not 
rated) provides gas primary system, hook-up and equipment. In China, Acter operates 
through Acter Technology Integration Group (603163 CH, not rated) and Suzhou Winmax 
(not listed) for Chinese EPCM (engineering, procurement, construction, and management) 
and equipment business. Except for C&S (civil and structural) works, where the margin is 
low (7-8%) and the contract duration is long, Acter’s services span nearly the entire 
cleanroom construction process. Its one-stop solutions model supports customer retention 
Acter provides 
integrated cleanroom 
solutions via various 
subsidiaries

### Page 7

7

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
and improves bid-win rates, particularly for overseas expansion, where customers have 
few reliable providers to choose from.

Acter: organisational chart

Source: Company, Daiwa

To avoid the risk of over-concentration, Acter diversifies its client portfolio across 
semiconductor foundries, memory, PCB, OSAT, ODM hardware assembly, CSP data 
centres and the consumer industry. By sector, semiconductor projects represent the largest 
share of Acter’s portfolio, accounting for 66% of total revenue in 1H26, vs. 69% in 2025. 
Foundry manufacturing is the largest source of demand for cleanrooms in Taiwan, 
especially as advanced-node capacity expands at sites in Hsinchu, Taichung and 
Kaohsiung. In the memory segment, the company executes turnkey engineering builds to 
expand its capacity aggressively, creating cleanroom opportunities across Taiwan, Japan 
and Singapore. This multi-sector coverage decreases the risk of cyclical downturns in any 
individual segment, in our view.

By establishing 33 operating entities across 11 international regions, the company 
effectively captures capex migration driven by global N+1 supply chain diversification. This 
allows Acter to support Taiwanese and international clients as they expand manufacturing 
capacity from Taiwan into Southeast Asia, Japan and the US. Due to the complexity of 
advanced-node IC manufacturing, packaging and testing, it is especially important for 
these Taiwanese semiconductor companies to work with familiar cleanroom builders when 
expanding in the US. This is one of the reasons why Taiwanese cleanroom companies are 
receiving overseas orders and starting to set up global bases, in our view. Despite higher 
management, material, and manpower costs in the US, most cleanroom companies are 
benefitting from the trend by improving margins, given the higher complexity and risks to 
operate overseas. Across overseas hubs in Texas and Phoenix (expected to begin from 
2027), Acter prioritises local hiring and partners with regional civil contractors to shorten 
the learning curve and to avoid the high overhead. To further address the potential labour 
shortages in the future, the company has established cross-border academic and 
vocational partnerships to bring Southeast Asian talent to Taiwan for technical training 
before deploying them back to their home markets, securing future human capacity to 
support global expansion projects.

By product, cleanroom electromechanical services remain Acter’s largest source of 
revenue (accounting for 66% of 1H26 revenue, per the company). These services include 
cleanroom architectural and MEP engineering, which powers airflow, filtration, 
environmental regulation and utility controls. Water, gas and chemical delivery systems are 
its second-largest source of revenue (accounting for 24% of 1H26 revenue, per the 
company). Contributed mainly by Novatech (6613 TT, not rated) and Rayzher (7703 TT, not 
rated), these services cover the main systems and hook-up systems, for  ultra-pure water 
(UPW), specialty gases, chemical piping, etc., all of which require highly precise build-up 
work. Except for cleanroom electromechanical and water, gas and chemical delivery 
systems, the rest of Acter’s revenue comes from the consumer industry services 
electromechanical (5%), biomedical (3%), and high-tech equipment and materials sales 
and services (1%).

Acter: revenue by product

Source: Company, Daiwa

Technically, Acter integrates digital transformation and green engineering into its business 
model, helping it differentiate from peers. Acter deploys digital twin technology and 1:1 3D 
BIM modelling to detect piping interferences and optimise spatial layout before installation, 
minimising schedule delays. This makes Acter a more reliable option, in our view, and thus 
being a favourable supplier, especially for the semiconductor clients that have extremely 
high quality and swiftness criterion.

Bright outlook: strong order visibility 
Rising orders on hand due to supply-demand imbalance for cleanrooms 
Acter's order backlog has increased from TWD55bn in 1Q26 to TWD60bn in 2Q26 and 
TWD66bn in July 2026. We expect this figure to further increase in the following quarters, 
given the supply-demand imbalance in the cleanroom sector. This is composed of 
semiconductor (63%), electronic assembly (11%), consumer (9%) and others (17%), per 
the company. In 2H25, the company accelerated the digestion of small-scale projects 
below TWD1bn, and, as a result, starting from 1H26, it successfully shifted its project

Back-end packaging, cloud infrastructure and hardware assembly are additional growth

qualified supplier status with US CSPs, providing mechanical, electrical and plumbing 
(MEP) integration, especially for those data centres that implement liquid-cooling systems. 
The company also wins factory construction projects for Taiwanese ODMs across the US 
and Southeast Asia, as well as PCB facility projects in Thailand and China. Non-tech 
sectors, including biotechnology, commercial buildings, smart logistics hubs and medical 
facilities, comprise 9% of the total backlog, per management.

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
Competitive advantages 
High entry barriers to the segment 
Comprehensive product portfolio, empowered by subsidiaries 
Acter provides a one-stop EPCM solution, leveraging its subsidiaries to deliver complete 
solutions. Within the group, the parent company Acter focuses on overall cleanroom and 
MEP system integration; subsidiary Novatech provides primary chemical systems and 
hook-up systems; and sub-subsidiary Rayzer specialises in primary specialty-gas systems 
and hook-up systems. In China, Suzhou Winmax, also a subsidiary of Novatech, focuses 
on the supply of wet processing and chemical handling equipment. This integrated model 
covers cleanroom engineering integration, water, power, chemical and gas-utility systems, 
as well as post-completion validation and maintenance, ensuring cross-system 
compatibility while mitigating risks associated with multi-contractor hiring.

Under global supply chain diversification trends, this unified group strategy strengthens 
order-winning capabilities in overseas markets. Clients expanding internationally face 
regulatory, linguistic and supply-chain complexities, favouring partners that offer one-stop 
services with a proven cross-border track record.

A diverse client base helps Acter weather economic cycles 
Acter’s main competitive advantage is its diversification of clients by industry and region. 
This guides the company through the peaks and troughs of the economy, which is 
especially crucial given the strong cyclicality of the semiconductor/high-tech industry.  Acter 
serves a broad client base across semiconductors, OSAT, PCB, electronics assembly 
(ODM), CSP data centres, biopharmaceuticals and even the consumer industry (including 
commercial real estate and logistics), whereas many peers rely on a few large foundry 
clients. When semiconductor demand slows, capacity expansion in the PCB or data centre 
sectors offset the decline. Additionally, Acter avoids concentration risk and margin 
compression from major foundry accounts, as projects from leading foundries yield lower 
margins; by serving niche markets like US-based CSPs, the company achieves higher 
gross margins.

Geographically, Acter operates across Taiwan, China, Southeast Asia, the US and other 
countries. The company has OSAT and foundry customers in Taiwan and China, PCB 
clients expanding into Thailand, and ODM and data centre clients establishing operations 
in the US. Across different countries, local hiring and local supply chain implementation 
can further reduce costs and increase efficiency relative to peers reliant on a cross-border 
labour force.

Labour shortages 
Amid global labour shortages, Acter minimises the operational impact and turns workforce 
constraints into a moat through localised talent development. Since engineering labour 
cannot easily be automated, Acter has built a cross-border talent pipeline via industry-
academia partnerships. The company recruits Southeast Asian students for specialised 
M&E engineering training in Taiwan, deploying them to global project sites after their 
graduation to solve labour bottlenecks. This structure maintains an engineering team with 
an average tenure of more than 7 years, creating an operational barrier in an industry with 
a high employee turnover rate.

Compared with peers relying on high-cost personnel sent from Taiwan, we note that Acter’s 
local hiring strategy creates cost and efficiency advantages. Prioritising compliant local 
hiring across overseas markets reduces travel expenses while accelerating integration with 
local supply chains and regulations. Localised teams also handle post-completion services 
and technical support directly on-site, building long-term client trust in overseas markets.

### Page 11

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
Numerous competitors, each with different specialisation 
Acter faces different competitors across its main product lines. In the EPCM segment, 
including cleanroom and MEP works, key competitors include UIS (2404 TT, not rated), 
L&K (6139 TT, not rated), Yankey (6691 TT, not rated) and Jiuhan (6903 TT, not rated). 
Despite having business models similar to Acter, UIS, Yankey and Jiuhan focus more on 
major semiconductor players in both Taiwan and the US, and L&K concentrates on ASEAN 
semiconductor projects. In the equipment/material sales and agency space, Acter mainly 
competes with Marketech (6196 TT, not rated). In the water, gas and chemical delivery 
systems area, through Novatech and Rayzher, Acter group competes with Marketech, 
Mega Union (6944 TT, not rated), Taiwan Puritic (6826 TT, not rated), Trusval (6667 TT, not 
rated) and Cica-Huntek (6725 TT, not rated).

In our view, despite the competitive environment, Acter maintains a formidable competitive 
moat built on several key advantages. We attribute this to its more than 45 year experience 
in the industry, enabling it to develop specialised expertise that is hard for competitors to 
replicate.

Financial analysis 
We forecast the company’s net profit to rise at a CAGR of 36.9% over 2026-28E, driven by 
a revenue CAGR of 24.6% due to surging demand from the high-tech industry (an increase 
in the order backlog to TWD66bn at the end of July 2026 vs. TWD46bn at end-2025) and a 
3.3pp expansion in operating margin over 2026-28E, driven by the gross margin 
improvement from more favourable project wins and operating leverage.

Strong order visibility, with revenue expected to grow 
across all sectors over 2026-28E 
We forecast Acter’s revenue to increase at a CAGR of 24.6% over 2026-28E, driven by 
scale expansion and a growing demand from various industries, including semiconductor, 
electronic assembly, optoelectronics and others.

Acter: revenue growth trend

Source: Company, Daiwa forecasts

Gross- and operating-margin expansion trend  
Over 2026-28E, we forecast Acter’s gross and operating margins to expand on rising 
revenue scale and a higher complexity of cleanrooms for advanced manufacturing. We 
note that the gross margin from high-tech cleanrooms is higher than that from consumer 
industries, such as commercial buildings and biopharmaceuticals. Thus, we estimate the 
company’s gross margin to reach 26.0% in 2028E from 18.9% in 2025 and its operating 
margin to reach 21.6% in 2028E from 13.7% in 2025.

Acter: gross- and operating-margin trend

Source: Company, Daiwa forecasts

Net margin likely to rise over 2026-28E 
We forecast Acter’s net profit to rise to TWD8,797m in 2027E and TWD11,851m in 2028E 
(from TWD6,325m in 2026E). We expect its net margin to come in at 13.1% in 2027E and 
14.2% in 2028E (vs. 11.7% in 2026E) and for its net profit to grow at a CAGR of 36.9% 
over 2026-28E.

Intact demand to support revenue growth over our 
forecast horizon; ROE expansion underway 
We forecast Acter’s total cash and equivalents to reach TWD16,838m (36.8% of its total 
assets) in 2026E and increase to TWD27,839m in 2028E (42.8% of total assets) with net

We believe the capacity expansion cost will initially be low and that the company will 
continue to benefit from the better profitability.

(following steady annual increases to 46.8% in 2026E and 54.9% in 2027E), which we 
attribute to margin expansion from the revenue size increase and gross margin expansion 
led by increases in cleanroom complexity.

Valuation and risks  
Undemanding valuation, alongside its leading global 
positions in the OSAT and memory segments and an 
optimistic industry outlook  
We initiate coverage of Acter with a Buy (1) rating and 12-month TP of TWD1,415, based 
on a target PER of 20x applied to our one-year-forward EPS forecast. This target multiple 
is higher than the average of its past-3-year 1-year-forward PER of 12.2x (ranged from 
7.4x to 23.5x) and the average of its past-5-year 1-year-forward PER of 9.6x (ranged from 
4.4x to 23.5x), which we view as undemanding as we forecast a 36.9% net profit CAGR 
over 2026-28E, implying a 0.54x PEG. This is supported by its business expansion in the 
next 2-3 years. We see potential for a rerating of the stock, given Acter’s global leadership 
in cleanroom EPCM, particularly in the OSAT and memory industries.

The stock is trading currently at a 15.8x 1-year-forward PER vs. its earnings CAGR of 
36.9% over 2026-28E, translating to a 0.43x PEG ratio. Its share price has underperformed 
TAIEX Index by 32.7% in 3Q26, mainly due to its weaker-than-expected 2Q26 results. 
However, given the potential improvement in sector sentiment following TSMC’s results on 
15 October 2026, along with the likelihood that Acter’s revenue momentum will strengthen 
in the coming months, we recommend that investors buy the stock.

Acter: past-3-year-forward PER (average: 12.0x)    Acter: past-5-year-forward PER (average: 9.5x)

Source: Bloomberg, Daiwa estimates  Source: Bloomberg, Daiwa estimates

We compare Acter with other leading cleanroom EPCM companies such as UIS (2404 TT, 
TWD1,330.0, not rated), L&K (6139 TT, TWD787.0, not rated) and Yankey (6691 TT, 
TWD776.0, not rated). UIS, L&K and Yankey are trading at a PER of 14.7x (earnings 
CAGR of 40.7% over 2025-27E), 9.5x (earnings CAGR of 36.9% over 2026-28E) and 
15.1x (earnings CAGR of 53.6% over 2025-27E), on their 1-year-forward EPS, 
respectively.

Acter’s peers are trading at 18.7x for 2026E and 14.2x for 2027E. We forecast Acter to 
deliver an EPS CAGR of 36.9% over 2026-28E vs. 31.5% CAGR for the segment over the 
same period.

We believe our target one-year-forward PER of 20x (equivalent to a 0.54x PEG using 
2026-28E EPS CAGR) is undemanding as: 1) Acter is ramping up capacity to meet 
growing demand from high-tech customers and is in an expansion cycle; and 2) its 
leading position among OSAT and memory clients warrants a valuation premium, 
compared to its global peers.

Risks to our call  
Primary/key risks: 
Weaker-than-expected expansion in semiconductor industry 
The biggest downside risk to our call for Acter is weaker-than-expected expansion from 
semiconductor clients, especially in advanced-node foundries, advanced packaging and 
OSAT. If global demand for AI servers, HPC or HBM slows, or if clients delay fab build-ups 
and overseas expansion plans, it will directly pressure order inflows and backlog 
conversion for Acter.

Project execution delays or cancellations 
Acter’s revenue recognition is tied to large, complex EPCM and integrated engineering 
projects, which are often subject to customer-driven delays. Any postponement in project 
Our 12-month TP of 
TWD1,415 is also 
supported by our DCF 
valuation

### Page 18

18

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
schedule, construction bottlenecks or cancellation of large projects could defer revenue 
recognition and hurt overall profit. This risk is more obvious in overseas projects, where 
licensing, fire-safety approvals and hazardous-chemical regulations are more complicated.

Higher-than-expected raw-material price hikes 
Raw-material costs (especially copper and oil-related costs) account for 50-60% of COGS 
and thus the price volatility makes costs difficult to manage. Acter needs to maintain 
supplier relations, negotiate appropriate price-adjustment mechanisms with clients, and 
invest in R&D to identify composite material substitutes, thereby reducing reliance on any 
single component.

Secondary risks:  
Labour issues or larger-than-expected increases in labour costs  
Cleanroom engineering is highly labour-intensive and relies on on-site installation and 
supervision. Although Acter has built a local hiring and talent-development model, a larger-
than-expected shortage of engineers or rising wage pressure could still raise costs and 
constrain capacity expansion. Any inability to secure enough skilled labour may also limit 
the company’s ability to convert orders into revenue in time.

Margin pressure from lower-margin projects or mix shift 
Acter’s business mix includes a few low-margin C&S services as well as larger EPCM and 
system integration contracts. If the company takes on a higher proportion of lower-margin 
projects, or if competition intensifies in certain geographies or customer segments, gross 
margin could come under pressure. In addition, an unfavourable product mix shift towards 
less profitable work could offset benefits from backlog growth.

Overseas expansion and regulatory risks 
Acter is expanding across markets in the US, Southeast Asia, Japan and China, which 
exposes it to FX movements, local compliance requirements, tax complexity and regulatory 
uncertainty. Delays in licensing, changes in safety standards or local-content requirements 
could increase project costs and reduce execution efficiency. Any misstep in managing 
overseas operations could damage profitability and client retention. 
Key risks to our call:

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
Appendix 
Company background 
History 
Acter (5536 TT), founded in 1979 and headquartered in Taichung, Taiwan, is a cleanroom 
EPCM provider with over four decades of experience. Starting with early mechanical and 
electrical (M&E) engineering through vertical and horizontal integration, Acter has now 
developed into an engineering technology group with cross-border execution capabilities. A 
cleanroom building process begins with civil & structural (C&S) works to establish the 
physical plant, followed by mechanical, electrical, and plumbing (MEP) works for 
essential utility generation, where Acter is one of the experts. After MEP is finished, 
chemicals/gas/water are then distributed via main systems through primary piping 
networks, and finally delivered to production equipment through hook-up systems. The 
company’s core competency is providing one-stop integrated total solutions, spanning 
services, primary piping networks and hook-up systems, leaving C&S services to other 
engineering peers, given the nature of the latter segment’s low margin and long timespan.

In terms of business model, Acter delivers EPCM (Engineering, Procurement, 
Construction, Management) capabilities. From initial customised planning and feasibility 
assessments to strategic procurement, installation, and post-completion maintenance, 
Acter builds production environments tailored to client process requirements.

Acter: EPCM solutions offered

Source: Company, Daiwa

Regarding global expansion, Acter actively responds to the global supply chain’s “N+1” 
strategy, operating across 11 regions with 33 companies globally. Its service network spans 
Taiwan, China, Southeast Asia (including Thailand, Malaysia, Vietnam, Indonesia, and 
Singapore), Japan, India, and the US. The company leverages a group synergy strategy 
by integrating resources from key subsidiaries – such as Novatech (6613 TT, not rated; 
provides chemical primary and hook-up systems), Rayzher (7703 TT, not rated; provides 
special gas primary and hook-up supply systems), and Acter Group (603163 CH, not rated; 
provides cleanroom engineering in China) – to form a complete technical ecosystem, 
ensuring cross-border capability and quality.
